        Idea:
Add idle-release feature for end-user local workers via a tiny stdlib Python supervisor entrypoint. Design: supervisor process (run_worker.py) launches the actual worker as a subprocess with byte-identical CLI args, env, cwd, and python interpreter. Worker has a new --idle-release-minutes flag (default 15.0, 0 disables) that only takes effect in personal-token mode (gated on resolved client_key from _resolve_worker_db_client_key, so service-key/cloud workers behave identically to today). When the worker has gone N minutes without claiming a task on the no-task branch of its poll loop, it cleanly flushes pending DB writes, closes heartbeat, and calls sys.exit(75). Supervisor sees exit code 75 and immediately relaunches the worker with the exact same args/env. Result: during idle, only the ~20MB supervisor process exists; worker process is fully gone, so all VRAM, CUDA context, RAM, file handles are released to the OS. CRITICAL REQUIREMENTS: (1) Idle timer must only start counting AFTER the worker has fully onboarded/started up (e.g. after first successful poll or explicit ready signal), NOT from process spawn — otherwise a slow startup could trigger immediate self-kill. (2) Onboarding-grace-period should itself be configurable via a CLI param. (3) Supervisor must reliably restart on exit 75 with no backoff (idle restart is expected, not a crash). (4) Supervisor must distinguish exit 75 (restart) from exit 0 (clean stop, do not restart) from signal-death like SIGINT (do not restart, exit with same code) from genuine crashes (restart with exponential backoff and a circuit breaker — max 5 crashes in 5 min then give up). (5) Supervisor must forward SIGINT/SIGTERM to the child so Ctrl+C works. (6) Service-mode workers (cloud, with SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY or WORKER_DB_CLIENT_AUTH_MODE=service) must NEVER trigger idle exit — behavior identical to today. (7) Idle exit must only fire on the no-task branch of the poll loop, never interrupting in-flight work. Tests: auth gate precedence, idle-decision helper (with onboarded gate), exit code semantics. Supervisor itself should be tested with a fake child binary that exits with controlled codes.

Exit-code semantics: rc==75 \u2192 log `'[SUPERVISOR] idle-release restart'`, restart immediately, NOT counted toward circuit breaker; rc==0 \u2192 return 0; rc<0 (POSIX signal death) \u2192 return `128 + (-rc)`, do not restart; other non-zero \u2192 append `time.monotonic()` to crashes deque, prune entries older than 300s, if `len(crashes)>=5` log `'[SUPERVISOR] circuit open after 5 crashes in 5 min \u2014 giving up'` and return last rc, else `time.sleep(min(2**(len(crashes)-1), 30))` and restart. After child exits, if `_shutdown_requested`, do not restart; return child rc (or `128+sig`). Total \u2264200 lines.",

Cannot get task.\")\n+        return ClaimPollOutcome.ERROR, None\n+\n+    # Worker ID is required\n+    if not worker_id:\n+        headless_logger.error(\"No worker_id provided to get_oldest_queued_task_supabase\")\n+        return ClaimPollOutcome.ERROR, None\n+\n+    headless_logger.debug(f\"DEBUG: Using worker_id: {worker_id}\")\n+\n+    # OPTIMIZATION: Check task counts first to avoid unnecessary claim attempts\n+    headless_logger.debug(\"Checking task counts before attempting to claim...\")\n+    task_counts = check_task_counts_supabase(\"gpu\")\n+\n+    if task_counts is None:\n+        headless_logger.debug(\"WARNING: Could not check task counts, proceeding with direct claim attempt\")\n+    else:\n+        totals = task_counts.get('totals', {})\n+        # Gate claim by queued_only to avoid claiming when only active tasks exist\n+        available_tasks = totals.get('queued_only', 0)\n+        eligible_queued = totals.get('eligible_queued', 0)\n+        active_only = totals.get('active_only', 0)\n+\n+        headless_logger.debug(f\"[CLAIM_DEBUG] Task counts: queued_only={available_tasks}, eligible_queued={eligible_queued}, active_only={active_only}\")\n+\n+        # Log warning if counts are inconsistent\n+        if eligible_queued > 0 and available_tasks == 0:\n+            headless_logger.warning(f\"Task count inconsistency detected: eligible_queued={eligible_queued} but queued_only={available_tasks}\")\n+            headless_logger.warning(f\"This suggests tasks exist but aren't visible as 'Queued' status - possible replication lag or status corruption\")\n+            # Proceed with claim attempt despite queued_only=0 since eligible_queued>0\n+            headless_logger.debug(f\"[CLAIM_DEBUG] Proceeding with claim attempt despite queued_only=0 because eligible_queued={eligible_queued}\")\n+        elif available_tasks <= 0:\n+            headless_logger.debug(\"No non-orchestrator queued tasks according to task-counts, still attempting claim (orchestrators excluded from counts)\")\n+            #         Project directory:

        Idea:
Add idle-release feature for end-user local workers via a tiny stdlib Python supervisor entrypoint. Design: supervisor process (run_worker.py) launches the actual worker as a subprocess with byte-identical CLI args, env, cwd, and python interpreter. Worker has a new --idle-release-minutes flag (default 15.0, 0 disables) that only takes effect in personal-token mode (gated on resolved client_key from _resolve_worker_db_client_key, so service-key/cloud workers behave identically to today). When the worker has gone N minutes without claiming a task on the no-task branch of its poll loop, it cleanly flushes pending DB writes, closes heartbeat, and calls sys.exit(75). Supervisor sees exit code 75 and immediately relaunches the worker with the exact same args/env. Result: during idle, only the ~20MB supervisor process exists; worker process is fully gone, so all VRAM, CUDA context, RAM, file handles are released to the OS. CRITICAL REQUIREMENTS: (1) Idle timer must only start counting AFTER the worker has fully onboarded/started up (e.g. after first successful poll or explicit ready signal), NOT from process spawn — otherwise a slow startup could trigger immediate self-kill. (2) Onboarding-grace-period should itself be configurable via a CLI param. (3) Supervisor must reliably restart on exit 75 with no backoff (idle restart is expected, not a crash). (4) Supervisor must distinguish exit 75 (restart) from exit 0 (clean stop, do not restart) from signal-death like SIGINT (do not restart, exit with same code) from genuine crashes (restart with exponential backoff and a circuit breaker — max 5 crashes in 5 min then give up). (5) Supervisor must forward SIGINT/SIGTERM to the child so Ctrl+C works. (6) Service-mode workers (cloud, with SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY or WORKER_DB_CLIENT_AUTH_MODE=service) must NEVER trigger idle exit — behavior identical to today. (7) Idle exit must only fire on the no-task branch of the poll loop, never interrupting in-flight work. Tests: auth gate precedence, idle-decision helper (with onboarded gate), exit code semantics. Supervisor itself should be tested with a fake child binary that exits with controlled codes.

fake_worker(\n+        [],\n+        env_keys=[\"RUN_ID\"],\n+        trap_sigterm=True,\n+        marker_path=str(marker_path),\n+        sigterm_exit_code=143,\n+    )\n+    repo_root = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]\n+    env = os.environ.copy()\n+    env[\"RUN_ID\"] = \"signal-case\"\n+    env[\"PYTHONPATH\"] = str(repo_root) + os.pathsep + env.get(\"PYTHONPATH\", \"\")\n+    code = textwrap.dedent(\n+        f\"\"\"\n+        from source.runtime import supervisor\n+        supervisor.WORKER_SHIM = {str(worker_path)!r}\n+        raise SystemExit(supervisor.main(argv=[\"--worker\", \"signal-case\"]))\n+        \"\"\"\n+    )\n+    proc = subprocess.Popen(\n+        [sys.executable, \"-c\", code],\n+        cwd=repo_root,\n+        env=env,\n+    )\n+    try:\n+        deadline = time.time() + 5\n+        while time.time() < deadline:\n+            if len(_read_launches(log_path)) >= 1:\n+                break\n+            time.sleep(0.05)\n+        else:\n+            raise AssertionError(\"supervisor did not launch child in time\")\n+\n+        os.kill(proc.pid, signal.SIGTERM)\n+        rc = proc.wait(timeout=5)\n+    finally:\n+        if proc.poll() is None:\n+            proc.kill()\n+            proc.wait(timeout=5)\n+\n+    assert marker_path.exists()\n+    assert rc == 143\n+    assert len(_read_launches(log_path)) == 1\n*** End Patch\n"
}

> DEVELOPER

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker

        Idea:
Add idle-release feature for end-user local workers via a tiny stdlib Python supervisor entrypoint. Design: supervisor process (run_worker.py) launches the actual worker as a subprocess with byte-identical CLI args, env, cwd, and python interpreter. Worker has a new --idle-release-minutes flag (default 15.0, 0 disables) that only takes effect in personal-token mode (gated on resolved client_key from _resolve_worker_db_client_key, so service-key/cloud workers behave identically to today). When the worker has gone N minutes without claiming a task on the no-task branch of its poll loop, it cleanly flushes pending DB writes, closes heartbeat, and calls sys.exit(75). Supervisor sees exit code 75 and immediately relaunches the worker with the exact same args/env. Result: during idle, only the ~20MB supervisor process exists; worker process is fully gone, so all VRAM, CUDA context, RAM, file handles are released to the OS. CRITICAL REQUIREMENTS: (1) Idle timer must only start counting AFTER the worker has fully onboarded/started up (e.g. after first successful poll or explicit ready signal), NOT from process spawn — otherwise a slow startup could trigger immediate self-kill. (2) Onboarding-grace-period should itself be configurable via a CLI param. (3) Supervisor must reliably restart on exit 75 with no backoff (idle restart is expected, not a crash). (4) Supervisor must distinguish exit 75 (restart) from exit 0 (clean stop, do not restart) from signal-death like SIGINT (do not restart, exit with same code) from genuine crashes (restart with exponential backoff and a circuit breaker — max 5 crashes in 5 min then give up). (5) Supervisor must forward SIGINT/SIGTERM to the child so Ctrl+C works. (6) Service-mode workers (cloud, with SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY or WORKER_DB_CLIENT_AUTH_MODE=service) must NEVER trigger idle exit — behavior identical to today. (7) Idle exit must only fire on the no-task branch of the poll loop, never interrupting in-flight work. Tests: auth gate precedence, idle-decision helper (with onboarded gate), exit code semantics. Supervisor itself should be tested with a fake child binary that exits with controlled codes.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker

        Idea:
Add idle-release feature for end-user local workers via a tiny stdlib Python supervisor entrypoint. Design: supervisor process (run_worker.py) launches the actual worker as a subprocess with byte-identical CLI args, env, cwd, and python interpreter. Worker has a new --idle-release-minutes flag (default 15.0, 0 disables) that only takes effect in personal-token mode (gated on resolved client_key from _resolve_worker_db_client_key, so service-key/cloud workers behave identically to today). When the worker has gone N minutes without claiming a task on the no-task branch of its poll loop, it cleanly flushes pending DB writes, closes heartbeat, and calls sys.exit(75). Supervisor sees exit code 75 and immediately relaunches the worker with the exact same args/env. Result: during idle, only the ~20MB supervisor process exists; worker process is fully gone, so all VRAM, CUDA context, RAM, file handles are released to the OS. CRITICAL REQUIREMENTS: (1) Idle timer must only start counting AFTER the worker has fully onboarded/started up (e.g. after first successful poll or explicit ready signal), NOT from process spawn — otherwise a slow startup could trigger immediate self-kill. (2) Onboarding-grace-period should itself be configurable via a CLI param. (3) Supervisor must reliably restart on exit 75 with no backoff (idle restart is expected, not a crash). (4) Supervisor must distinguish exit 75 (restart) from exit 0 (clean stop, do not restart) from signal-death like SIGINT (do not restart, exit with same code) from genuine crashes (restart with exponential backoff and a circuit breaker — max 5 crashes in 5 min then give up). (5) Supervisor must forward SIGINT/SIGTERM to the child so Ctrl+C works. (6) Service-mode workers (cloud, with SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY or WORKER_DB_CLIENT_AUTH_MODE=service) must NEVER trigger idle exit — behavior identical to today. (7) Idle exit must only fire on the no-task branch of the poll loop, never interrupting in-flight work. Tests: auth gate precedence, idle-decision helper (with onboarded gate), exit code semantics. Supervisor itself should be tested with a fake child binary that exits with controlled codes.

Exit-code semantics: rc==75 \u2192 log `'[SUPERVISOR] idle-release restart'`, restart immediately, NOT counted toward circuit breaker; rc==0 \u2192 return 0; rc<0 (POSIX signal death) \u2192 return `128 + (-rc)`, do not restart; other non-zero \u2192 append `time.monotonic()` to crashes deque, prune entries older than 300s, if `len(crashes)>=5` log `'[SUPERVISOR] circuit open after 5 crashes in 5 min \u2014 giving up'` and return last rc, else `time.sleep(min(2**(len(crashes)-1), 30))` and restart. After child exits, if `_shutdown_requested`, do not restart; return child rc (or `128+sig`). Total \u2264200 lines.",

        Idea:
Add idle-release feature for end-user local workers via a tiny stdlib Python supervisor entrypoint. Design: supervisor process (run_worker.py) launches the actual worker as a subprocess with byte-identical CLI args, env, cwd, and python interpreter. Worker has a new --idle-release-minutes flag (default 15.0, 0 disables) that only takes effect in personal-token mode (gated on resolved client_key from _resolve_worker_db_client_key, so service-key/cloud workers behave identically to today). When the worker has gone N minutes without claiming a task on the no-task branch of its poll loop, it cleanly flushes pending DB writes, closes heartbeat, and calls sys.exit(75). Supervisor sees exit code 75 and immediately relaunches the worker with the exact same args/env. Result: during idle, only the ~20MB supervisor process exists; worker process is fully gone, so all VRAM, CUDA context, RAM, file handles are released to the OS. CRITICAL REQUIREMENTS: (1) Idle timer must only start counting AFTER the worker has fully onboarded/started up (e.g. after first successful poll or explicit ready signal), NOT from process spawn — otherwise a slow startup could trigger immediate self-kill. (2) Onboarding-grace-period should itself be configurable via a CLI param. (3) Supervisor must reliably restart on exit 75 with no backoff (idle restart is expected, not a crash). (4) Supervisor must distinguish exit 75 (restart) from exit 0 (clean stop, do not restart) from signal-death like SIGINT (do not restart, exit with same code) from genuine crashes (restart with exponential backoff and a circuit breaker — max 5 crashes in 5 min then give up). (5) Supervisor must forward SIGINT/SIGTERM to the child so Ctrl+C works. (6) Service-mode workers (cloud, with SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY or WORKER_DB_CLIENT_AUTH_MODE=service) must NEVER trigger idle exit — behavior identical to today. (7) Idle exit must only fire on the no-task branch of the poll loop, never interrupting in-flight work. Tests: auth gate precedence, idle-decision helper (with onboarded gate), exit code semantics. Supervisor itself should be tested with a fake child binary that exits with controlled codes.

Implement `def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int`: launch child via `subprocess.Popen`; install SIGINT/SIGTERM handlers in main() that capture `child.send_signal(sig)` and set `_shutdown_requested=True` (Windows fallback: `child.terminate()`); track crashes via `deque`. Exit-code semantics: rc==75 \u2192 log `'[SUPERVISOR] idle-release restart'`, restart immediately, NOT counted toward circuit breaker; rc==0 \u2192 return 0; rc<0 (POSIX signal death) \u2192 return `128 + (-rc)`, do not restart; other non-zero \u2192 append `time.monotonic()` to crashes deque, prune entries older than 300s, if `len(crashes)>=5` log `'[SUPERVISOR] circuit open after 5 crashes in 5 min \u2014 giving up'` and return last rc, else `time.sleep(min(2**(len(crashes)-1), 30))` and restart.         Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker

        Idea:
Add idle-release feature for end-user local workers via a tiny stdlib Python supervisor entrypoint. Design: supervisor process (run_worker.py) launches the actual worker as a subprocess with byte-identical CLI args, env, cwd, and python interpreter. Worker has a new --idle-release-minutes flag (default 15.0, 0 disables) that only takes effect in personal-token mode (gated on resolved client_key from _resolve_worker_db_client_key, so service-key/cloud workers behave identically to today). When the worker has gone N minutes without claiming a task on the no-task branch of its poll loop, it cleanly flushes pending DB writes, closes heartbeat, and calls sys.exit(75). Supervisor sees exit code 75 and immediately relaunches the worker with the exact same args/env. Result: during idle, only the ~20MB supervisor process exists; worker process is fully gone, so all VRAM, CUDA context, RAM, file handles are released to the OS. CRITICAL REQUIREMENTS: (1) Idle timer must only start counting AFTER the worker has fully onboarded/started up (e.g. after first successful poll or explicit ready signal), NOT from process spawn — otherwise a slow startup could trigger immediate self-kill. (2) Onboarding-grace-period should itself be configurable via a CLI param. (3) Supervisor must reliably restart on exit 75 with no backoff (idle restart is expected, not a crash). (4) Supervisor must distinguish exit 75 (restart) from exit 0 (clean stop, do not restart) from signal-death like SIGINT (do not restart, exit with same code) from genuine crashes (restart with exponential backoff and a circuit breaker — max 5 crashes in 5 min then give up). (5) Supervisor must forward SIGINT/SIGTERM to the child so Ctrl+C works. (6) Service-mode workers (cloud, with SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY or WORKER_DB_CLIENT_AUTH_MODE=service) must NEVER trigger idle exit — behavior identical to today. (7) Idle exit must only fire on the no-task branch of the poll loop, never interrupting in-flight work. Tests: auth gate precedence, idle-decision helper (with onboarded gate), exit code semantics. Supervisor itself should be tested with a fake child binary that exits with controlled codes.

Exit-code semantics: rc==75 \u2192 log `'[SUPERVISOR] idle-release restart'`, restart immediately, NOT counted toward circuit breaker; rc==0 \u2192 return 0; rc<0 (POSIX signal death) \u2192 return `128 + (-rc)`, do not restart; other non-zero \u2192 append `time.monotonic()` to crashes deque, prune entries older than 300s, if `len(crashes)>=5` log `'[SUPERVISOR] circuit open after 5 crashes in 5 min \u2014 giving up'` and return last rc, else `time.sleep(min(2**(len(crashes)-1), 30))` and restart.
